# Ledger shortlist for Flight Console

The 196 features the scouts said would suit this lineage (118 of them gems), in full. The whole menu is ledger-index.md.

### L001 Deep-set tube in a lifted case (colour or material, GEM)
From **01-softground Soft Ground**, round 1 (4.0, #12 of 12).
- What: The case round each monitor is lifted a step (`.crt` gradient #28322d to #202823 to #1b221d, surface #1d2421) while the glass stays today's pitch black (glass mix on #060a08). Each screen reads as a dark tube sunk into a lighter surround, like a real IBM 5151, rather than a black box on a black page.
- Files: Palette: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/palettes/01-softground.json (surface steps; screen and glass untouched). Sheet: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/out/01-softground/style.css line 1142 `.crt` background gradient, line 1139 `--phosphor-glass`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/out/01-softground/shots/login.png (the sign-in face in its lifted case; lost.png too)
- Praise: Soul 4.5: "the deep monitors set in the lighter surround are a nice touch." Its designer: "the screens look even more like dark tubes set into the page." Otherwise last place.
- Caveats: It got there by lifting the whole ground, which greyed the black into 'stock slate dark mode' in every lens. Lift only the case or the bay that holds a tube, and keep the page at #0b0e0d so the green still jumps off a switched-off screen.
- Suits: Switchboard: the rack's cells in a lighter slate, each holding black glass. Flight Console: grey console bays with the tubes sunk into them. Track: the slate diagram board round the Next up tube. Figure Ground: the figure's screen sunk into its plate.

### L002 Stacked glass panes with glinting edges (colour or material, GEM)
From **06-bottleglass Bottle Glass**, round 1 (4.7, #10 of 12).
- What: Surfaces are drawn as sheets of dark bottle glass. Each layer up is lighter, more chromatic and a little greener (bg #051010 at h196, surface #0a1c1c, surface-2 #0f2322, surface-3 #152b29 at h188). The hairlines carry more chroma and a greener hue than the faces (line #1c3632, line-2 #284641, edge #557873), so card and bubble borders glint like the edge of a pane, and the family's bubbles become lighter teal panes.
- Files: Palette: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/palettes/06-bottleglass.json (bg to surface-3, line, line-2, edge, ink). Sheet: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/out/06-bottleglass/style.css :root tokens, line 1427 `.said-them .bubble`, `.panel`, `.card`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/out/06-bottleglass/shots/login.png (the sign-in card as a pane; chat.png for the teal bubbles)
- Praise: Style 6: "the boldest and most memorable ground, stacked panes of bottle glass with glinting edges, and it clearly has an idea." Product 5: "tinted glass surfaces and teal chat bubbles look premium at first." Its designer measured hue entropy up 45-100% on every page.
- Caveats: Applied to the whole page it turned everything teal: 'the deep sea' in place of 'the Matrix', one colour family, and the green jumping less (feedback 4.5, skeptic 3). Use it on one family of objects over the green-black page, as a lens or a single readout panel, and keep the page's neutrals as they are.
- Suits: Switchboard: each cell's lamp as an illuminated glass lens, a pane that brightens when lit. Flight Console: the readout strip as a layered glass instrument window. Track: the lamp lenses on the diagram. Pocket: the handheld's screen glass in layers over its four greens.

### L003 The brand is never a kind (colour or material, GEM)
From **08-colourmonitor Colour Monitor**, round 1 (5.5, #7 of 12).
- What: Outings stop sharing the signature green. Outing gets its own colour, turquoise #43dbd0 (the light cyan of an EGA screen), so any green on an idea card or in 'Lately added' means the brand and nothing else. The rest of the kind set takes a colour monitor's phosphors, deeper and more saturated rather than pastel: ideas violet #b188fe, plans sky #6ac4fb, to do yellow #f6d94f, people #fdae4f, shows #fd82ba, seasons #fc8d5e. Every CVD distance improves (kinds 13.6 to 15.8 typical, 5.2 to 9.2 protan). Pine and Alpenglow's glacier-lake teal outing (#65d1da, round 2) is the same move.
- Files: Palette: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/palettes/08-colourmonitor.json (outing, ideas, plans, todo, people, shows, seasons). harness/theme.py rewrites `.kind-outing { --kind: var(--outing); }` and `:has(.kind-outing) { --accent: var(--outing); }`. Round 2 variant: rounds/r2/palettes/r2-rand-1.json (outing #65d1da).
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/out/08-colourmonitor/shots/home.png (Lately added: the turquoise Outing label)
- Praise: Product 6.0: "The best idea as a system for a family app ... outings get their own turquoise so the brand stops doubling as a kind ... Keep the turquoise outing and the richer sections, which are a real gain for the system." Product notes' synthesis: "08's outing with its own colour, so the brand is never a kind." Style, round 2, on Pine: "keep the glacier-teal outing, which is lovely."
- Caveats: In small labels only, it is invisible at a glance (feedback 5). Turquoise can read as a second blue beside trips' sky, or as more green to some eyes. The tritan pair show~seasonal fell to 5.4. The round 2 lesson says moving learned section colours costs points, so change outing and leave the sections the family knows. Lantern Night's persimmon outing showed that warm hues work for a kind only as small solids.
- Suits: All six have kind marks. Track: a stop's lamp ring in its kind colour, never green. Figure Ground: kind symbols in the figures' legends. Flight Console: the crew board's kind tags. Switchboard: the Ideas cell's breakdown by kind. Timetable: the enamel line colours stay apart from the phosphor. Pocket: the cartridges' labels.

### L004 Two inks: phosphor for the machine's voice, blue-black for the family's hand (colour or material, GEM)
From **r4-rand-3 Kitchen Clock**, round 4 (5.56, #7 of 12 in round 4).
- What: One rule for colour by author. Kitchen Clock limits blue-black ink to what the family writes and touches: fields (#0b121a), date tiles and 'in N days' pills (#1b2633), their chat bubbles, links (#79a0c5) and the current tab. Cards, lines and the case stay the room's colour, and the machine's things stay green. Sonar Watch states it as 'the family's hand is ink, the machine's is phosphor', with a logbook ink (--log #141d27, --log-edge #263444, --log-ink #9eb4ca) on the family's bubbles, names, times and date leaves. Composing Room puts it in the names: 'Vera' in sage mono on her lines, the family's names in blue pencil.

Sonar Watch: rounds/r4/palettes/r4-rand-4.json `:root { --log:#141d27; --log-edge:#263444; --log-ink:#9eb4ca }`, `.said-them .said-by, .said-them .said-by strong { color: var(--log-ink); }`, `.chip { background: var(--log); border-color: var(--log-edge); }`, `.chip-dow { color: var(--log-ink); }`. Composing Room: rounds/r4/palettes/r4-idea-2.json `.said-bot .said-by strong { color: var(--lit); }`. Markup: `chat_line` and `chip` macros in src/familydb/web/templates/_ui.html (lines 69 and 119).
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r4/out/r4-rand-3/shots/home.png (blue-black date tiles and pills in Coming up, the field in the box). Also rounds/r4/out/r4-rand-4/shots/chat.png and rounds/r4/out/r4-idea-2/shots/chat.png (sage 'Vera'). Crops: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/120fcf6f-20be-59ef-8e10-e5252598c703/scratchpad/s34/kc_chat.png, sw_chat.png and cr_chat.png.
- Praise: Interaction 6.4 (Kitchen Clock): 'A clear meaning: blue-black is what the family writes or touches (fields, date tiles, bubbles, links, the current place), which gives the best type-here signal of the field.' Feedback 5.6 (Sonar Watch): 'the blue logbook ink is kept to what the family touches'. Soul 7.6 (Composing Room) kept 'sage mono for Vera's name'. The design's claim: 'In the chat you can tell who is speaking by material as well as by side.'
- Caveats: In Kitchen Clock it sat inside moss cards, which made 'a second temperature at the point of writing' (system 5.9, skeptic 4.3), so it needs a neutral slate or green-black room. In Sonar Watch 'the blue marks are too small to count'. Keep the ink low in chroma (blue-black, not navy), since navy cards failed repeatedly. Decide where the Home box sits: it is on the greeting's glass, so its field can stay glass. Flight Deck (round 9) had the same code, 'cyan means entered by the family', and lost for other reasons; keep it off Tokyo Night's cyan.
- Suits: timetable: round 7 found that 'Chat as a one-sided log loses mine versus hers'. With two inks, Vera's entries are phosphor and sage (her name lit) and the family's are blue-black ink with their names and times, so the timetable can drop bubbles and still tell speakers apart. track: plans the family entered are inked onto the diagram, and the machine's lamps are phosphor. console: the crew board and every value the family set in ink, readouts in phosphor.

### L007 Glaze: a solid lit from above that pools dark at its foot (colour or material, GEM)
From **r5-rand-5 Kettle and Glaze**, round 5 (5.46, #7 of 12).
- What: The things the family presses or owns (the ways to start, the family's chat bubble, the date leaves, the current tab and place) are a dense lacquer-like solid. A soft white sheen sits on the top 60% and an inset shadow pools at the foot, so the key reads as moulded and lit from above without any glow. On the phone the ways to start become chunky glazed keys.
- Files: Palette /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r5/palettes/r5-rand-5.json (css): token `--glaze: linear-gradient(180deg, rgb(255 255 255/.07), rgb(255 255 255/0) 60%)` layered as `background: var(--glaze), var(--lacquer)` on `.said-them .bubble`, `.starters a`, `.chip`, `.tabs a[aria-current="page"]` and `.places a[aria-current="page"]`, plus `.starters a, .said-them .bubble, .chip, .tabs a[aria-current="page"]{box-shadow: inset 0 1px 0 rgb(255 255 255/.07), inset 0 -3px 6px -2px rgb(0 0 0/.45)}`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r5/out/r5-rand-5/shots/home-phone.png (ways to start)
- Praise: Style 6.6: 'glazed petrol bubbles and chips feel crafted.' Interaction 5.8: 'the clearest selected-state pill (petrol glaze) and the strongest separation between the family's bubbles and Vera's.'
- Caveats: The petrol hue (h200, near Solarized base02) is what failed, so lay the glaze over slate (#1d2330) or pewter. Keep it to what the family presses and owns: on every surface it becomes Front Panel's keycap costume. It must stay flat enough that a family bubble does not look like a button (a round 5 lesson).
- Suits: Pocket Timeline: the handheld's moulded plastic keys and the family's side of the screen read as toy plastic without neon. Videotex Switchboard: the key row along the foot as glazed keys. Flight Console: the console's push-buttons.

### L009 Grained cabinet case (rebuild as brushed metal) (colour or material, GEM)
From **r7-rand-3 Headland Light**, round 7 (5.01, #11 of 12).
- What: Every monitor sits in a real-looking cabinet: a 97-degree repeating-gradient grain over a three-stop vertical gradient, a chin, a vented grille drawn as a 3rem strip of 2px bars, and a brand-green power light with a small halo. The case becomes a material object with a surface, not a grey rounded rectangle.
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r7/palettes/r7-rand-3.json css: `.crt { padding:.75rem .75rem 2.2rem; border-radius:26px 26px 32px 32px; background-image: repeating-linear-gradient(97deg, rgb(0 0 0/.07) 0 2px, transparent 2px 7px, rgb(255 255 255/.025) 7px 8px, transparent 8px 13px), linear-gradient(180deg,#57413a,var(--walnut) 45%,#33241f); border-color:#5e4840; box-shadow: inset 0 1px 0 rgb(255 255 255/.12), inset 0 -3px 7px rgb(0 0 0/.35), 0 30px 50px -30px rgb(0 0 0/.95), 0 0 70px -40px color-mix(in srgb,var(--phosphor) 40%,transparent) }`, `.crt::before` (grille: `repeating-linear-gradient(90deg, rgb(0 0 0/.45) 0 2px, transparent 2px 5px)`), `.crt::after` (power light with `0 0 6px color-mix(in srgb,var(--brand) 70%,transparent)`).
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r7/out/r7-rand-3/shots/login.png (also ideas.png)
- Praise: soul 4.9: 'The walnut cabinets with grain and chin are an authentic 70s touch.' style 5.6: 'The walnut cabinets on the monitors ... are charming and have real personality.' The style notes list 'Headland Light's cabinet grain (on Sign in and 404 only)' among the seed traits worth one more try.
- Caveats: As wood it failed: 'kitsch' (skeptic, system, lessons.md), 'steampunk rather than period CRT' (feedback 3.2), and brown, which the owner rejects. Keep the technique, not the wood. Use a fine horizontal brushing (0-2deg, 1px lines every 3-4px at 2-4% light and dark) over a graphite or pewter gradient (#4a4f55 to #34383d). Use it only on the one monitor that stands alone (Sign in, 404, or Home's Next up), so it reads as the machine's body and not as texture all over the page.
- Suits: console: brushed-aluminium bays and bezels, true to a 1969 flight console, and a material that carries grounded colour without paint. switchboard: the Minitel's moulded plastic case around its terminal, a fine texture in place of the brown keys the lineage lost. pocket: the handheld's plastic shell, drawn thin, instead of the chunky beveled cartridges.

### L010 Green-bar banding, rows and weeks (colour or material, GEM)
From **r7-rand-6 Orchard Greenbar**, round 7 (5.53, #8 of 12).
- What: Every list the family reads (Coming up, To do, Status lights, the kids' wishes, the data tables) sits in a flat wall-less tray. Its odd rows are banded a step lighter, like the green-bar listing paper of the CRT era, so the rows drop their hairlines and pack tighter. On the Plans month, alternate weeks are banded the same way, like a printout.
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r7/palettes/r7-rand-6.json css: `:root { --band:#18201d }`, `.panel.plain, .monitors > .panel, .home-wishes, .spend { background: var(--surface); border-color:transparent; box-shadow:none }`, `.plain > li:nth-child(odd), .data tbody tr:nth-child(odd) td { background: var(--band) }`, `table.month { background: var(--surface); border-color:transparent; box-shadow:none }`, `table.month tbody tr:nth-child(even) td:not(.outside):not(.today) { background: var(--band) }`, `.home-wishes > .plain > li { background: var(--band) }`. There is no variant folder: CSS only.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r7/out/r7-rand-6/shots/status.png (also plans.png for the week bands)
- Praise: soul 5.8: 'Green-bar listing paper is a genuine CRT-era artefact'. system 6.0: 'Green-bar banding is a period-true, tasteful idea'. The soul notes say that, tried once more, it 'belongs on slate trays, not sage ones'. lessons.md round 7: 'green-bar banding on slate trays, not sage' is worth one more try.
- Caveats: In sage the room read as green on green, 'more monochrome, not less' (feedback 5.2, skeptic 4.9). The bands were 'too faint to register' (style) and 'vanish on a dim screen' (interaction). Rebuild on a slate tray with a visible step of about 2.5-3 dE (for example tray #10161a and band #151c21), keep 44px rows, and use it only where rows are compared (lists, tables, the month). Do not also box each row.
- Suits: timetable: a printed timetable is banded, so band its timetable rows and Chat's times margin. track: the Plans month in week bands under the diagram, and Status's rows. console: Slate Flight Console already bands its rows; its month can take the week bands. switchboard: the sommaire's list of pages.

### L012 Warm Bakelite for the monitor case only (colour or material, GEM)
From **r9-mut-3 Bakelite Video**, round 9 (5.66, #9 of 13).
- What: The monitors' case is drawn in one warm material, oxblood Bakelite (#3a2826, C about .025, hue 15-22). It has a warm sheen (an #ffeee6 top highlight), an inner shadow at the chin, grille slots cut into the chin and the green power LED. The case is thinner and squarer than before (padding .6rem .6rem 1.9rem, radius 18/18/22/22), with flush 14px and 8px scopes on Status and Ideas. The judges' own fix points the same way: keep the material, but use less of it.
- Files: variants/r9-mut-3/palette.css: `.crt{padding:.6rem .6rem 1.9rem;border-radius:18px 18px 22px 22px;box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 #ffeee626,inset 0 -3px 7px #0005,0 5px 0 -1px #221313,0 36px 56px -30px #000e,0 0 80px -38px color-mix(in srgb,var(--phosphor) 40%,transparent)}`, `.crt::before{left:1.3rem;bottom:.8rem;width:2.8rem;height:.3rem;background:repeating-linear-gradient(90deg,#0006 0 2px,transparent 2px 5px)}` (grille), `.crt::after{right:1.35rem;bottom:.74rem;width:.42rem;height:.42rem;background:var(--brand)}` (LED), `:is(.spend,.idea-scope) .crt{padding:10px;border-radius:14px}`. Token bezel #3a2826 in palettes/r9-mut-3.json "tokens".
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r9-mut-3/home.png (the Next up case)
- Praise: Style, 6: "The most material mood on the panel ... Keep the idea of a material; use less of it." Soul's fix: "If Bakelite is kept, keep it only in the monitor case (one material with a job)". Style's fix: "Keep the Bakelite as a frame and chrome material (bar, window frame lines, monitor case)". Skeptic's fix: "Oxblood only where there is Bakelite (the monitor case, perhaps the foot)." Soul, 5.6, against: "The brown cases make the tubes look like a 1970s TV cabinet".
- Caveats: Every lens marked down the oxblood filling windows, bubbles and the month (squinted, it read as Christmas). Feedback recalls that the owner said brown and warm tones do not match the phosphor, so the case is the most this material should cover. Lower the chroma further, or try a darker umber-graphite first. It answers 'more grounded coloration elsewhere' only while it stays small and solid, a material with a job.
- Suits: console: the tube bezels as the one warm material in a slate cockpit, like the Bakelite knobs of older avionics. figure: the frames round figures. switchboard: the cabinet round the map's Vera cell. timetable: Vera's own screen tile. It suits track least, since that lineage is steel.

### L014 Rules that take their part's colour (colour or material, GEM)
From **r9-rand-2 Porcelain Atlas**, round 9 (5.00, #11 of 13).
- What: One line of CSS tints every hairline on the page toward the colour of the part it belongs to. `* { --rule: color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 26%, var(--line)) }` resolves --rule on each element from that element's own --accent. Plans' rules lean teal, To do's straw, Ideas' violet, and the bar and Home cobalt, with no rule written per section.
- Files: r9-rand-2.json "css": `* { --rule: color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 26%, var(--line)); }`. Every border that uses var(--rule) picks it up: `.bar{border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule)}`, `.section-head{border-bottom:1px solid var(--rule)}`, `.tabs`, `.cards:not(.wishes){border-top:1px solid var(--rule)}`, `.said-bot .bubble`, `.settings-nav ol`, `.foot`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r9-rand-2/home.png (the head rules of The kids' wishes, Coming up, To do and Lately added)
- Praise: Soul's fix: KEEP "the rules tinted in each part's colour". System's fix: "Keep the rule tinted by its own section's colour". Skeptic's fix: keep "the star colours on rules and discs".
- Caveats: It works only where --accent is set per section or per kind; the harness already does this. Inside kind-tinted rows a rule may pick up the kind's colour; scope it with :where() if that is not wanted. At 26% the tint stays grounded; above about 35% it becomes the confetti feedback warned about.
- Suits: All six lineages, as one cheap system rule. figure: each figure's axis and tick marks lean to its section's colour. track: steel track lines lean to the colour of the section they serve. switchboard: each cell's frame leans to its page's colour. console: plate demarcation lines. pocket and timetable: the spine or thread stays steel, and each entry's rule takes its part's colour.

### L015 The page's line colour run along the bar's lower edge (colour or material, GEM)
From **r10-wild-3 Night Timetable**, round 10 (6.55, #12 of 19).
- What: A 3px band along the bottom edge of the sticky bar takes the current page's line colour (lilac on Ideas, blue on Plans, teal on To do, yellow on Status), like the colour band along a platform. On Home, where every line meets, the band is split into five equal segments, one per line. The footer repeats the five as short 2.25rem segments at its top rule, as a colophon.

### L017 Drawing-sheet material: graph paper under icons and date leaves (colour or material, GEM)
From **r11-mut-3 Blueprint Terminal**, round 11 (6.71, #10 of 17).
- What: A --sheet token draws a small blueprint drawing sheet: a 6px grid of 1px lines at 15% blueprint blue over #162030, with a 60% blueprint edge. It sits under every date leaf (1 OCT THU), row icon inlay and section-head tile, so the family's things are drawn on paper while the machine is glass.
- Files: .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-mut-3/palette.css --sheet, --sheet-edge (lines 35-36), used at 449 (heads), 489 (inlays), 508 (date leaves 4.25rem), 839

- Praise: Style: 'the blueprint-grid tiles give a specific texture'. System: 'a blueprint tile heads every row and every head (about twenty identical squares)'. Feedback: 'more blue than necessary'.

- Suits: figure: graph paper under each figure (Figure Ground's blueprint language). console: a plotting-board ground for the flight plan. track: the diagram drawn on a sheet. switchboard: cell tiles.

### L018 Cased lens with an engraved legend strip and a dot-matrix raster (colour or material, GEM)
From **r11-wild-1 Pocket Timeline**, round 11 (5.11, #17 of 17).
- What: Each green screen sits in a slate plastic lens with a big bottom-right radius (10px 10px 42px 10px), like a handheld's screen. Across its top runs a trim strip of two thin coloured lines with a small engraved legend in a gap ('PHOSPHOR DISPLAY'). A power LED sits in the bezel's left margin. Inside, the tube adds a fine dot-matrix grid over the scanlines, so the glass reads as an LCD matrix.
- Files: .../harness/rounds/r11/variants/r11-wild-1/sheet.css .crt (lines 1156-1172: gradient slate, border-radius 10px 10px 42px 10px, 80px phosphor spill), .crt::before (the trim strip and legend, 1174-1190), .crt::after (power LED, 1192-), .crt-tube::after (the dot-matrix raster, line 1231)
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r11-wild-1/home.png (crop x 130-790, y 545-800)
- Praise: Soul 5.0: 'Keep ... the phosphor display in its slate bezel'. The design's tubes carry the kit's effects in full.
- Caveats: 'PHOSPHOR DISPLAY' at 8px is costume. Replace it with a legend that says something (the scale, as Long Persistence's chin does), and keep it aria-hidden. Retune the berry and indigo trims to steel hairlines. Test the raster at 1x so it does not muddy VT323.
- Suits: console: the instrument case, with the legend strip naming the readout ('SPEND · LIMIT $2.00'). switchboard: every cell's small screen in one asymmetric lens. figure: a figure plate cased as one screen. pocket: its own.

### L025 Two inks for every printed figure, by relative colour (colour or material, GEM)
From **r13-wmut-1 Folio Switchboard**, round 13 (6.88, #9 of 17).
- What: Every printed figure (counts, drive times, due leaves, initials, day numbers) is coloured by its owner (a kind, a section or a role), but at one fixed value: --fig-ink is oklch(from owner 0.68 c*0.92 h). Folios use --folio-ink at L 0.55 and chroma times 0.72. Relative colour keeps each hue exact and its chroma, where a colour-mix toward the green-tinted graphite rotated heather toward blue and rose toward salmon. A row of figures in different hues reads as one set of inks. With prefers-contrast: more both inks switch to full colour.
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r13/variants/r13-wmut-1/sheet.css --fig-ink and --folio-ink (lines 115-116), the five figure steps --f-xs to --f-xl (66-70), prefers-contrast override (2276). Palette: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r13/palettes/r13-wmut-1.json
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r13-wmut-1/ideas.png
- Praise: not remarked by the judges (the critic asked for a single ink; the designer's relative-colour version is the refinement).
- Caveats: Relative colour syntax needs Chrome 119+, Safari 16.4+ or Firefox 128+; give a color-mix fallback. The folio ink is 3.3:1, fine only for large decorative numerals.
- Suits: All six. It makes grounded owner colour even and calm wherever kinds and sections print a figure (the owner's 'more grounded coloration elsewhere'): figure, switchboard and console most of all.

### L033 Two-track tape chat with the time printed on the tape (component, GEM)
From **r10-wild-1 Braun Signal**, round 10 (5.48, #16 of 19).

Vera's lines sit on the left track beside her voice mark and the family's on the right beside their initial, with a 56px spine between them. The time is printed on the tape under each mark like a tape counter ('27 sep / 19:47'), so side, mark and time each tell the speakers apart, not colour alone. ### L049 Raised, latched and sunk control grammar (control, GEM)
From **r9-rand-1 Attract Mode**, round 9 (5.04, #10 of 13).
- What: One affordance rule covers every control. Raised means press it: the green primary key has a 3px darker skirt and moves down 3px on :active, and the graphite secondary keys, including the band-head links 'All plans →' and 'All 4 →', have a 2px skirt. Sunk means type here: every field is a well with a darker top edge and an inset shadow. A flat inlay means it only labels: heading icon caps, date chips and initials have a 1px edge and an inner top highlight but no skirt. The mic is a round, dished arcade button.
- Files: r9-rand-1.json "css". Tokens in :root: `--key:linear-gradient(180deg,#293938,#1c2b2a)`, `--key-edge:#384c4c`, `--key-depth:inset 0 1px 0 rgb(255 255 255/.07),0 2px 0 #030706,0 4px 10px -4px rgb(0 0 0/.8)`, `--key-down:inset 0 1px 3px rgb(0 0 0/.55),0 0 0 #030706`, `--well:inset 0 2px 4px rgb(0 0 0/.55),0 1px 0 rgb(255 255 255/.05)`, `--inlay:inset 0 1px 0 rgb(255 255 255/.08)`. Blocks: "Keys: every button is a key with a little travel" (`button,.button{... box-shadow:inset 0 1px 0 rgb(255 255 255/.5),0 3px 0 color-mix(in srgb,var(--green) 35%,#000),...}`, `button:active{transform:translateY(3px)}`); "Section links are small keys" (`.section-head a,.section-links a,.brief-more a{background:var(--key);border:1px solid var(--key-edge);box-shadow:var(--key-depth)}`); "Wells: every box you type in is sunk into the panel"; `button.mic{border-radius:50%;background:radial-gradient(circle at 50% 38%,#2b3c3b,#172322 70%)}`; `button.tick::before` and `.task-box` drawn as wells. The latched state: `.tabs a[aria-current="page"],.settings-nav a[aria-current="page"]{background:var(--deck);box-shadow:var(--key-down);transform:translateY(2px)}`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r9-rand-1/home.png (Send, 'All plans →', 'All 4 →', the starters and the wells). ideas.png shows the backwards latch.
- Praise: System, 5.6: "The clearest affordance language in the field: keys with travel you press, sunk wells you type in". Interaction, 4.5: the band-head keys are "the most obviously pressable links on the panel". System's fix: "Keep the affordance grammar and breed it into the slate system: raised keys = press, sunk wells = type in, latched = here. It is the clearest usability idea this round." lessons.md: "its keys and wells had the clearest affordance".
- Caveats: The keys were about 36px, and 41 of 56 Home targets were under 44px; set min-height to 2.75rem. The latched 'here' reads backwards: on Ideas and Plans the chosen tab looks sunk and off while the other looks raised and lit (interaction, usability). Make the chosen key the lit one, filled or with a lit 2px cap, and keep 'sunk' for fields only. The focused select lost its ring; keep the 2px #6dff9c ring on every control. Style disliked the pill-shaped head keys ('stock SaaS'), so a lineage that prefers links can keep only the wells and the Send key.
- Suits: console: cockpit pushbuttons; raised keys, with the engaged one latched and lit by a lamp or EFIS cyan. switchboard: every page cell is a key you press, with its lamp in it and the current page's key latched and lit. track: a signal box's route keys and lever frame. timetable and pocket: take the wells for the ask box and the raised Send key only.

### L050 Numbered bar where every place is named, with a solid reverse cell for 'here' (control, GEM)

All ten places are named, none as a bare icon, and the numbers match each page's chapter and its FIG. and plate numbers (1.2 Coming up, FIG. 1.2). ### L060 Graduated scale with an index needle (drawing or diagram, GEM)
From **r7-rand-4 Pewter Dome**, round 7 (5.69, #7 of 12).
- What: Under each page title (and in Pewter Dome under every section head and the month head) runs an instrument scale made of pure CSS gradients: a 1px baseline, minor ticks every 6px, mid ticks every 30px and major ticks every 60px, fading out to the right. A 3px by 15px index needle in the page's own colour stands at the left end. A shorter scale, fading at both ends, sits above the footer. It is the scope's ticked rim laid flat.

The scale': `.page-head, .section-head, .month-head { position:relative; padding-bottom:.85rem }`, `::after { position:absolute; inset:auto 0 0 0; height:7px; background: linear-gradient(var(--tick-hi),var(--tick-hi)) 0 100%/100% 1px no-repeat, repeating-linear-gradient(90deg,var(--tick-hi) 0 1px,transparent 1px 60px) 0 100%/100% 7px no-repeat, repeating-linear-gradient(90deg,var(--tick-hi) 0 1px,transparent 1px 30px) 0 100%/100% 5px no-repeat, repeating-linear-gradient(90deg,var(--tick) 0 1px,transparent 1px 6px) 0 100%/100% 3px no-repeat; mask-image: linear-gradient(90deg,#000 45%,transparent) }`, `.page-head::after { height:10px }`. Needle: `.page-head::before, .coming > .section-head::before, .lately > .section-head::before { left:0; bottom:0; width:3px; height:15px; border-radius:2px 2px 0 0; background: var(--lead, var(--accent)) }`. Footer: `.foot::before` (a 5px scale masked `transparent, #000 20%, #000 80%, transparent`). Tokens `--tick:#3a3535; --tick-hi:#574f4f`.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r7/out/r7-rand-4/shots/status.png (page head); home.png for section heads
- Praise: soul 5.3: 'the graduated scale under the heads and the graticule rings in the corner are real scope touches'. system 6.2: 'a graduated scale under every head as one consistent rule'. style 6.1: 'regular-weight Newsreader titles on graduated tick scales'. The style notes suggest it again 'under the page title only, not every head'.
- Caveats: Under every head it read as 'a perforated edge' (skeptic 4.6, interaction 5.6, system), and lessons.md lists it among the traits that faded. Use it once per page, under the masthead, or at half opacity (type fix), in steel rather than warm greige. Make the needle mean something: set its position from data (spend against the limit, the share of the week gone, the next due item), and light it #6dff9c with the kit's halo only when it shows a live reading.

Put one under each console bay's legend, with the needle as the bay's reading (LATE, spend), so it joins the readout strip and the annunciator. figure: the axis of a figure drawn from data, for example To do's due dates as ticks on the scale with NOW as the lit needle, which is the figure the lineage promises. track: a day ruler under the fortnight line.

### L062 Time dial with signal bursts and a now needle (Next up, and the Plans month header as a tuner) (drawing or diagram, GEM)
From **r10-wild-1 Braun Signal**, round 10 (5.48, #16 of 19).

On Plans (month) it becomes the month's header: 'september 2026 / 1 day with plans' at the left of a slim dial of the month's days, with bursts and the needle on today, flanked by Earlier and Later keys, so the header works as a tuner. On 404 it is a flat line with no signal.

### L065 Scope trace of the coming four weeks (drawing or diagram, GEM)
From **r10-wild-2 Bench Scope**, round 10 (5.98, #15 of 19).
- What: On Home's Next up tube the family's next 28 days are drawn as a logic-analyser trace on a scope graticule. The baseline runs from now at the left edge, where the scope 'triggers': a small triangle on the top of the glass, a dashed line down to the axis and a triangle on the axis. Each day with a plan gets a square pulse, one height step per plan that day (c1/c2/c3). The next pulse is intensified the way a scope brightens the part of the sweep it is looking at, and its day number glows. A month turn is marked on the axis ('OCT'), a plan past four weeks shows an arrow at the right edge, and the timebase '1 wk/div' is printed. The day arithmetic is done in the template (days_from_civil), so the view needs nothing new.

- Suits: Switchboard: the literal switchboard, so Status's cell or page as this board, each connection a jack with its lamp. Console: the systems page of the annunciator. Track: the fallback chain and connections as a signal diagram. Figure: its own lineage, carried on.

### L089 Mint core, deep emerald bloom (light effect, GEM)
From **03-emeraldaura Emerald Aura**, round 1 (4.5, #11 of 12).
- What: Every bright core keeps today's #6dff9c, but its bloom is a deep saturated emerald (#12a472, hue 162, L 0.64) instead of a whitish mint fog. That is how a real tube blooms: darker and richer than the phosphor at its centre. The multiplier goes up (glow 1.15) because emerald carries a third of the mint's luminance, so the net light is 24-29% lower but reads as depth. It covers the key word, Send's ring and pool, the mark's tile, live lights, the box's focus and the greeting's pool. The tube glass takes an emerald breath too (screen-glass #04120c).

### L092 Light from below: a bottom-weighted inset glow (light effect, GEM)
From **r3-rand-3 Rose Embers**, round 3 (4.46, #9 of 12 in round 3).
- What: Cards drop their outer shadow for an inner light weighted to the bottom edge, so each panel looks lit from beneath the way the greeting is lit by its screen. Setup, restaurants and trouble panels glow amber. On hover the glow rises in the card's own kind colour. The greeting was left out so its green pool stayed clean. The judges saw only a pink underglow on every card. The technique itself answers two of the main line's open problems: the greeting's lost 'lit pool from below' and the row afterglow on hover that has been 'invisible in stills' for five rounds.

### L105 Light spilling out round a cut in the page (light effect, GEM)
From **r12-wild-1 Glow Ink Zine**, round 12 (4.52, #17 of 17).
- What: Wherever the machine is live (Next up, the Ideas radar, her screen, sign-in), the tube is not a pasted-on monitor. The page is cut open over it. Its wrapper carries four stacked drop-shadows: a 0.7px and a 1.2px bone-white edge, like a torn fibre catching the light, then 26px and 70px phosphor spills. So the green leaks out onto the surface round the opening, and the opening has a pale lip. Inside the opening the kit is complete: scanlines, a sheen, a vignette, a 0.35px blur on the text lines, and reverse-video labels kept crisp above the scanlines.

- Praise: Not remarked by the judges; stills cannot show it.
- Caveats: .45s is too short to be noticed. Try steps(4,end) over about 1s, and pair it with the round 13 recipe (a 10-14% steel wash with a 2px lit-sage edge) so a mid-fade frame registers. Wine zebra and rose needle must go. Reduced motion should turn it off. It is an alternative to Reverse Video's smooth fade; choose one per standard.

### L109 Cold-tube warm-up when a page opens (motion, GEM)
From **r8-rand-2 Tidepool Felt**, round 8 (5.29, #11 of 12).
- What: When a page loads, each block of main arrives dim and grey like a cold tube (opacity .35, brightness .45, saturate .4, contrast .8) and warms to full over .8s, staggered .08s per block down the page. The mark and the lit word 'mind' come up through dim green and overshoot to brightness 1.3 at 55% before settling. The fill mode is backwards and it ends on filter:none, so no block is left as a layer over the menu.

- Praise: Not remarked by the judges; stills cannot show it.
- Caveats: Warming every page on every navigation will tire a family that moves between pages often. Run it on Home, or once per session, and let the monitors keep their own switch-on. Reduced motion must skip it (it did). filter on large blocks is costly on old phones, so keep the overshoot to the phosphor (the mark, 'mind', the now lamp). It must not delay reading: .8s at most.

It runs once and does not loop; nothing else on the page moves.

- Praise: Soul, 5.3: "the roll bar that sweeps the tube at power-on is the best CRT behaviour in the round". Soul's fix begins "KEEP: the roll bar ... (a true CRT behaviour, brief and one-shot)". Soul's notes: "the best tube behaviour anyone has drawn". lessons.md round 9, FAILED REACHES (3): "Attract Mode's power-on roll bar was the best tube behaviour of the round ... keep both."

### L156 Type features keyed to who is speaking, a slashed zero and a written scale (typography, GEM)

- What: One custom property, --ot, feeds font-feature-settings and is set by role. The family's words (Plex Sans) take "ss01","ss02", the schoolbook single-storey a and g. It replaced about forty sizes.

It is invisible at page scale on its own; it is a system to apply, not a look.
- Suits: Every lineage's type system. Timetable: the two voices of the conversation (the family's schoolbook, the machine's engineered) are exactly its speakers. ### L163 Width axis as the type hierarchy (typography, GEM)
From **r12-mut-1 Signal Legend, Widths**, round 12 (6.62, #10 of 17).
- What: One variable face, Archivo (wdth 62-125), carries the scale by width. The larger a title, the narrower it is set: page titles and the greeting's question at 70%, section heads at 80%. Everything read, pressed or typed is at 100%. The tiny capital labels, the page's only capitals, are set wide at 115% with +.06em tracking, like engraved nameplates. Condensed titles are set larger to keep their mass: h1 at clamp(2.35rem, 4.9vw, 3rem), weight 640, tracking -.006em. Plex Mono figures take the slashed zero.

Templates: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r12/variants/r12-mut-1/templates.

- Praise: type 6.6, with a promise vote: 'the idea is elegant: one variable face whose width axis carries the hierarchy'. system 6.8: 'A clean typographic rule... It is worth grafting'. feedback 7.3: 'a thoughtful width-axis type system... which the owner's love of typesetting rewards'. lessons.md: 'judged elegant and worth grafting'.
- Caveats: Archivo 400 body is paler than Plex: set text at 420-440, or keep Plex Sans for long text. At 115% the wide caps date rivals Vera's name: use 105-108% and .04em. The slashed zero blurs to an '8' in blooming VT323 ('$0.00', '17:00'), so use it on Plex Mono only, never on the glass. The change is subtle at page scale, so pair it with a structural change.
- Suits: timetable: a condensed timetable face for times and titles, with wide engraved labels. track: wide engraved caps like signal-box lever plates, against condensed diagram labels. console: condensed readout heads and wide placards. It works as a type system for all six.

### L166 The glass set as a terminal's lines, with double-height headlines (typography, GEM)
From **r13-mut-1 Track Diagram, Monospaced**, round 13 (6.32, #13 of 17).

### L175 Amber as the sibling phosphor, on glass only (colour or material)
From **07-ambercompanion Amber Companion**, round 1 (6.3, #2 of 12).
- What: The green's period sibling keeps it company. Honey amber #fbbe61 took the links, title tiles, panel icons, the phone's Home pill and the light from above. On Status, honey icons beside green live lights read as two kinds of light with two meanings. The soul judge's sharper idea: make the near-limit spend screen the true honey-amber phosphor. So the device is an amber tube, a `.crt` whose --phosphor is amber, kept for one meaning (money, caution, needs a look) beside green tubes that mean live.

Pocket and Timetable: a late row or a delayed departure in amber phosphor.

### L177 Warm solids, cool light (colour or material)
From **r2-idea-1 Lamplit Room**, round 2 (6.56, #4 of 12).
- What: A rule for where each temperature may go. Warmth is only ever opaque: ivory ink (#ddd5c4, #bfb9ab, #9c988e, #8b887e), moss-warm cards and bubbles (#131710, #191d16, #1e221b, field #0e120d), warm-stone hairlines (#2b2b24, #35352d, edge #6e6c62) and a warm monitor case. Cool colour is only ever light: an azure wash (#3d9bff at washGlow 2.6), a blue afterglow under the bar (#5a9cff at topGlow 1.5) and sky links (#7ad3ea). Nothing warm is ever diluted, so nothing samples bronze, and the room reaches below the fold on Home, Chat and Status.

### L185 EFIS colour code: one meaning per colour (colour or material)
From **r9-idea-3 Flight Deck**, round 9 (7.44, #3 of 13).
- What: Every colour has one meaning on every page, after the 1982 EFIS glass-cockpit code (itself drawn on a CRT). #6dff9c means active or live: the mark, Send, 'mind', focus, live lamps, the tubes and the NOW box. Sage means on or done. Cyan #83bfec means selected or entered by the family: links, the 1.5px selection box for 'here', family names in label lines, the edge of their chat lines and their initials. Bone is for legends, titles and data, and neutral grey for labels and computed data. Amber means caution only and red warning or off only; there is no magenta. The code is written at the top of the sheet.

- Praise: Soul's notes: "Flight Deck's EFIS colour code is the best-argued system for what each colour means since round 1". Style, 7.5: "An inspired reframe: a 1982 glass cockpit was a CRT, so the EFIS code gives every colour one job". Skeptic, 7.4: "each colour has one meaning". lessons.md candidate principle 18.
- Caveats: The cyan sat about 5 dE from Tokyo Night and read as an editor's link blue (skeptic, style); move it to #8fb3d6-#9cc3da (C about .06). Family names in cyan looked clickable (system, feedback); set names in bone with only a cyan initial or edge. The amber 'OPEN' tag and the amber 'Something to eat' plate broke amber's one job. It answers the skeptic's fix on Evening Listing: 'Give the steel here block a 2px lighter cap so it does not read as disabled.' Interaction: 'the steel reverse here is clear'.
- Caveats: Ink on it must stay dark (#0b0e0c) for about 5.5:1. Don't add a halo, since 'here' is not live. On the phone Relit swaps to a tinted cell with a 2px cap; keep one drawing of 'here' across sizes if possible.
- Suits: Switchboard: the current page's cell latched and backlit on the map. Console: the selected display-page key. Every lineage's bar and tabs; track can use it for today's stop.

### L188 Lamp board: each light in its own designator cell (component)
From **r6-idea-2 Radar Room**, round 6 (7.59, #2 of 12).
- What: On Status every line (Chat messages, OpenAI, Weather and travel…) is a slate strip. Its lamp sits centred in a square 3.5rem designator cell at the left, divided from the name and detail by a 1px cell rule, and the strips are racked on a 2px pewter rail. Read down a column, the lamps line up in one vertical line like a lamp board. The macro says each light's state three ways: colour, shape (lit dot, hollow ring, half) and the words beside it.

### L190 Speaker and time set into the family slip's frame (component)
From **r8-idea-2 Reverse Video**, round 8 (7.86, #2 of 12).
- What: On Chat, each family line is a square steel slip (#1b2126 fill, 1px #4c5760 frame, 2px radius). The speaker's name (bold Plex Mono, bone) and the time (Plex Mono) are set into the slip's top frame line at the right, the way a text-mode window carries its title. The masking band is split half window colour, half slip colour, so the line stops cleanly on both sides of the words. So mine and hers are told apart by material (square steel slip against round lit glass) as well as by side, and the byline takes no row inside the bubble.

Draw the square well with a ::before, or pick a radius that leaves about .35rem showing, so a box to tick stays a box (principle 17). Check that the enlarged areas do not overlap a neighbouring link in dense rows.
- Suits: All six, as the standard tick. It matters most for Flight Console's crew boards and annunciators, Videotex Switchboard's cells and Pocket Timeline's handheld rows, where the wild entrants lost on 41-53 small targets rather than on their ideas.

### L212 A scope that draws the family's weeks (drawing or diagram)
From **r11-idea-2 Long Persistence**, round 11 (7.88, #2 of 17).
- What: Next up's tube is a 4:3 P1 scope in a thin graphite case. Under the VT323 text, the trace covers the next four weeks at one week per division: a bright trigger at NOW on the left edge, one pulse per day with plans (one step taller per plan), day numerals, a dotted month-turn cursor, and the next plan pinged. A sweep beam crosses every 8s with a wake of afterglow, and each mark flares as the beam passes, then settles to a resting glow.

### L229 Lit run of track: white-hot core held, then fading into the steel (light effect)
From **r13-mut-4 Track Diagram Aglow**, round 13 (7.06, #7 of 17).
- What: Where a section of line is occupied, a run of #6dff9c with a 1px white-hot core (--hot) holds for 65% of the way and then fades to transparent, so the steel rail shows through before the next stop. Its bloom is filter: drop-shadow(0 0 2px brand) drop-shadow(0 0 8px brand/45%), which follows the run's own alpha, so the light falls off with the line and never glows along the steel. Live lamps breathe outward: core and tight bloom hold, and the wide halo swells from 48% to 56% and back, so a still never catches them dim.
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r13/variants/r13-mut-4/src/phosphor.css: --hot, --lamp-core, --lamp-glow (E20 numbers), --run-v and --run-h gradients (lines 15-27); .now-lamp and @keyframes lamp to --lamp-swell (96-97); .now-run, .line > li > .now-run, .now-stop::after, the Status chain li::before (105-162); forced colours (200). src/relight.css; templates home.html, tasks.html, plans.html add an empty span.now-run (aria-hidden).
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r13-mut-4/home.png (crop: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/120fcf6f-20be-59ef-8e10-e5252598c703/scratchpad/crops/aglow_run.png)
- Praise: feedback 8.9 (ranked it first): 'the lit run'; 'the halo back only on the live list'; soul 7.6: 'the run of track from NOW is visibly lit'. Other lenses: a near-duplicate of its parent at page scale.
- Caveats: Alone it is too small a change to see at page scale (0.1-0.9% of pixels). The soul lens asked for a travelling afterglow wake running from the NOW lamp toward the next stop. It needs an extra decorative span where a pseudo-element is already used.
- Suits: pocket: the spine lit from NOW to NEXT and fading into LATER. timetable: her line lit from the latest stop. console: the flown leg of the flight plan. figure: the NOW mark on To do's due line. track: its own.

### L230 The P7 flash: violet on arrival, green afterglow (motion)
From **r2-idea-2 Radar Flash**, round 2 (6.3, #5 of 12).
- What: The companion colour is justified as phosphor light itself. A radar's P7 tube lit each echo with a brief blue-violet flash, and a long green afterglow stayed on after it. In the palette the flash appears as an indigo-violet light in the greeting (#7d68ff at washGlow 2.6, clean at (34,34,56)), an afterglow under every bar (#7270f8 at topGlow 2.0), violet links and pill (#aea3fd), and a deep on-hue green halo (#2eac66). The device worth taking is the sequence: whatever is new arrives violet-white and decays to the steady green.

Sheet: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r2/out/r2-idea-2/style.css lines 131 (html), 892 (.welcome), 402 (phone pill). The motion is not built. It would be a keyframe on the radar's blips and on lamps (templates/_ui.html macros `radar`, `crt`), from a violet-white #b9b0ff with a wide text-shadow or box-shadow to var(--phosphor) over about 1.2s, the page's own --afterglow timing (style.css line 82).
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r2/out/r2-idea-2/shots/home.png (the flash in the greeting; ideas.png for the orchid Ideas tile)
- Praise: Soul 7.3: "The most CRT-true idea on the table: the companion is itself phosphor light (the P7 tube's violet flash)." Style 6.5: "The P7-tube story is the cleverest and most period-true rationale of the round."
- Caveats: As a static palette it moved least below the fold (mean dE 0.65, skeptic), so it repeated 'nearly identical'. Violet beside neon green is Dracula-adjacent (6.5 dE from #bd93f9). Make it meaning and motion, not tint: flash only on arrival (a new message, a plan just saved, a lamp that has just changed), once, and under prefers-reduced-motion show the settled green. Keep violet off the links.
- Suits: Flight Console: an annunciator lamp that has just changed flashes violet-white, then holds green, the way a master caution draws the eye. Switchboard: a cell whose count has just changed flashes once on its lamp. Track: the lamp at the stop just reached flashes and then burns steady. Night Timetable: a departure-board row that has just changed flips with a violet flash. Figure Ground: a figure's newest point flashes, then joins the drawing. Pocket: the NOW lamp as time passes a stop.

### L232 Highlight bar that fades like phosphor (motion)
From **r8-idea-2 Reverse Video**, round 8 (7.86, #2 of 12).
- What: Rows, Settings menu entries, bar menu items and Settings cards light at once (0s) on hover or focus-within, with a 16% steel wash and a 2px steel bar on the left edge. When the pointer leaves they fade over 1s on cubic-bezier(.3,.15,.4,1). The designer's motion frames show a row still half lit at 380ms and gone at 900ms. It is the tube's afterglow following the pointer down a list.

The base stylesheet's reduced-motion rule turns the fade off.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r8-idea-2/ideas.png (the unboxed rows it walks down; the motion itself is not in stills)

- Caveats: Row afterglow has been invisible in stills for five rounds (the round 13 open problem). The round 13 recipe is close to this: a 10-14% steel wash with a 2px lit-sage (#86d9a6) edge, fading over about 1-1.2s. Keep the green ring on the link itself. It must also run on :focus-within so keyboard users see it.
- Suits: All six. It is the cheapest way to put the tube's afterglow back into lists without adding glare: Evening-style rows, the track's stops, Switchboard cells and Flight Console log rows.

### L235 Newspaper column rules between Status's open columns (page structure)
From **r4-idea-2 Composing Room**, round 4 (6.83, #2 of 12 in round 4).
- What: On Status, the three panels (Who answers, Keys, Connected to) lose their boxes and are set as open columns under thick-and-thin head rules. A 1px vertical column rule sits in the middle of each 2.5rem gutter, from the head rule's foot to the last item, as in a newspaper. The rule appears only at 72rem and wider, so a wrapped column never draws a stray line. The page reads as a set page of three stories, not three cards.
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r4/palettes/r4-idea-2.json, css `@media (min-width: 72rem) { .monitors { column-gap: 2.5rem; } .monitors > .panel + .panel::before { content:""; position:absolute; top:1.1rem; bottom:.2rem; left:-1.25rem; border-left:1px solid var(--line); } }` together with the open-panel rule `.panel.panel:where(:not(.card, .idea-card, .setup, ...)) { background: var(--head-rule); border:0; border-top:2px solid var(--rule); ... }` and `:root { --rule:#4a5159; --head-rule: linear-gradient(var(--rule), var(--rule)) left 0 top 3px / 100% 1px no-repeat }`. Markup: src/familydb/web/templates/status.html `.monitors` (line 52).
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r4/out/r4-idea-2/shots/status.png
- Praise: Type 8.0: 'Status has column rules'. Feedback 6.5: 'open ruled columns on Status'. Soul 7.6: Status 'as ruled columns'. Skeptic 5.9: 'ruled Status columns. It changes structure rather than tint.' Style 7.3: 'Coming up, To do and Status sit open on the black, which gives the green-on-black signature more room'. Type fix: add a hairline between columns where they wrap below 72rem. System fix: show the column rule from 52rem.
- Caveats: The box-versus-set split hangs on a long :not() list; use an explicit `.set` class (asked by four lenses). Below 72rem the columns need a top hairline instead (`@media (max-width:71.99rem) { .monitors > .panel + .panel { border-top:1px solid var(--line); padding-top:1rem } }`). Round-5 lessons: structure alone drains colour, so pair set columns with a faint slate column fill. The thick-and-thin head rule itself already spread to the main line (the Oxford rule); the column rule is the part that did not.
- Suits: timetable: a timetable is columns under rules, so column rules between time, what and who, and between days, turn the chat-as-timetable into a real printed timetable. figure: figures set side by side as stat columns separated by column rules, like a newspaper's box score. console: the instrument columns of the annunciator and crew board.

### L241 Window legend set into the frame line (page structure)
From **r8-idea-2 Reverse Video**, round 8 (7.86, #2 of 12).
- What: Every read section is a flat steel field (#171d20) with a 1px frame drawn .6rem inside it by ::before. Its heading is set into the top line after a 1.25rem stub, with the section icon in its section colour, the only place the section colour appears. The way on ('All plans ->', 'All 4', 'Add an idea / All 12', 'Change a key') is moved after its list and set into the bottom-right line. Tabs (All ideas | Restaurants, List | Month) become the window's legend, with the current tab in reverse video. Any generic .panel becomes a window, so Family, Memory, Restaurants and the idea pages inherit it with no markup of their own. It is period grammar carried by line, not by paint.

Rules: `.panel{--fr:var(--frame);position:relative;padding:1.7rem 1.35rem 1.5rem;background:var(--win);border:0}`, `.panel::before{content:'';position:absolute;inset:var(--inset);border:1px solid var(--fr);pointer-events:none}`, `.panel>h2:first-child,.panel>.section-head:first-child>h2,.panel>.legend-icon:first-child,.panel>.eyebrow:first-child{position:relative;display:flex;width:fit-content;height:1.2rem;margin:0 0 1rem .5rem;padding:0 .5rem;font:600 .88rem/1.2rem var(--mono);letter-spacing:.07em;text-transform:uppercase;background:var(--win)}`, the icon `color:var(--lead,var(--label))`, `.panel>.section-head>.tabs`, and the bottom-frame `.win-foot` (`.panel:has(>.win-foot){padding-bottom:2.2rem}`). Tokens --inset .6rem, --frame #384149. Templates in rounds/r8/variants/r8-idea-2/templates/: home.html, ideas.html, plans.html, plans_month.html, status.html, tasks.html, wishes.html.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r8-idea-2/home.png (Coming up, To do and Lately added windows; crop /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/120fcf6f-20be-59ef-8e10-e5252598c703/scratchpad/crops/rv_coming.png); ideas.png and plans.png for the tab legends
- Praise: Skeptic (7.5): 'The only newcomer whose rules would hold on any page'. Style (7.5): 'the most CRT-native idea: the page is drawn in the grammar of Turbo Vision-era software, not in more green'. Soul (8.2): 'the most CRT personality of the field without adding a drop of green'. Round 9's Signal Legend carried it as rail legends.
- Caveats: Legends at .88rem/600/.07em mono caps shout ('THE KIDS' WISHES'). Take 500 .78-.8rem at .03-.05em, or sentence-case Plex Sans 600 .95rem for section names with mono caps only for small labels. Links and tabs set into the frame read as labels (interaction). Keep 'All ...' at the head's right as an underlined sentence-case link. Keep tabs as keys with a 44px hit area and a visible edge on the unselected one. Use one frame per window: the .6rem matte plus the inset line made Bootstrap fieldsets. At 18.7% green it was the least green entrant on Status, so give the legend icon or a lamp in the legend lit sage. Tab order of the moved links was never verified.
- Suits: Videotex Switchboard: each page cell becomes a window with its number and name in the top frame and its lamp and count in the bottom frame. Flight Console: bays with the legend engraved into the bay's frame line instead of a caps heading on top. Figure Ground: plates with 'FIG 3' and the plate's name set into the frame. Night Timetable: timetable boards titled in the frame.

- Praise: Interaction, 7: "Its bracket is the best head idea on the panel: title, tabs, 'Save a thought' and 'Add an idea' sit in one row, and To do's Add, Show and Search share one line, so every list starts sooner." Interaction's fix: "The bracket that gathers a head's controls beside its title is worth making standard on every page: it shortens the path from title to action to content." Usability's fix: "Keep the compact heads".
- Caveats: Holding the whole To do form inside the bracket costs about 500px on a phone (system, feedback); keep only Show, Search and Filter in it. Buffer stops read as pins, and a ring round every icon read as a generic badge (system, skeptic, style); end a head with nothing to hold in a plain tick. The Ideas and To do heads crowded their titles (type).
- Suits: track: part of its own first line language; revive it with these fixes. console: a panel head that gathers the page's switches under one bracket. switchboard: the page a cell opens, with its controls gathered under its head. figure: a figure's controls (tabs, filter) under its bracket.

### L246 Two lights, two meanings (live vs go there) (state marking)
From **10-lilac Mint and Lilac**, round 1 (7.1, #1 of 12).
- What: On the pages with no colour of their own (Home, Vera, Status, Settings), the accent leaves the green: links, the title tile, the panel-heading icons, the current nav icon and the phone's Home pill become a soft lilac (#c6aef3). On Status, green then means working (the live dots, the spend monitor), lilac means you can go there, and red rings mean off: three meanings a glance can split. The phone's lilac Home pill (the accent at 16% behind the icon) was the best tab state of round 1. Round 2's Evening Study made it crisp with orchid #d797f0 at C 0.14 (pill (74,56,81); Status green share 86.7% to 57.8%).

Sheet: round1/out/10-lilac/style.css line 468 `.title-icon`, 516 `.panel > h2 .icon`, 402 `.places a[aria-current="page"] .icon { background: color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 16%, transparent) }`, 1650 `.light.on` (green).

- Praise: Product 7.5: "The phone's lavender Home pill is the nicest tab state of the twelve." Skeptic 7: "the only one where the companion is plainly visible, complementary and clean at low alpha." Its designer: "green now means working and lilac means go somewhere. That split is legible and calm." Evening Study, round 2 winner, style 7.6: "a clear orchid for everything you follow."
- Caveats: At low chroma lilac went grey-mauve in the pill and on Status (round 2: 5.5). Lilac collapsed onto the Plans cyan for deutan sight (2.3 dE). Orchid with neon mint is Dracula's pairing, so a warm room has to keep it apart. Warm companions (amber, coral, rose, brass) beside the red rings read as warnings, so the 'go there' colour must be cool. Keep lavender out of the hairlines, which read as two temperatures.
- Suits: Switchboard: each cell's lamp green when live, its number and name in the go-there colour. Flight Console: lamps lit green for live, legends and links in the companion. Figure Ground: Status's wiring figure with lit green ports and links as the companion. Track: lamps green, station names and 'All plans' in the companion. Timetable and Pocket: the same rule on every page, Settings included.

### L250 Double frame for what you act in, lit while you type (state marking)
From **r8-idea-2 Reverse Video**, round 8 (7.86, #2 of 12).
- What: The line weight carries meaning. What you read is a single-frame window. What you act in (the ask box, the filters, forms, Settings groups, pickers, the bar menu) is a #1b2126 dialog in a 3px double frame (#4c5760). A form's heading sits on the double line on a 4px band of the outside colour. While a text field, textarea or select inside is focused, the whole double frame turns lit sage (#86d9a6), but not on button focus, so the form you are in lights up as one unit.
- Files: rounds/r8/palettes/r8-idea-2.json css. Rules: `.panel:is(form,details),.dlg,.ask,.save-bar,.desk-words,.settings-body form .panel{background:var(--dlg);border:3px double var(--frame-2);border-radius:2px;box-shadow:0 1px 0 #0007}`; `:is(.panel:is(form,details),.dlg,.settings-body form .panel,.ask,.desk-words):has(:is(input:not([type=checkbox],[type=radio]),textarea,select):focus){border-color:var(--lit)}`; heading on the line: `:is(.dlg,.settings-body form section.panel)>h2:first-child{--o:var(--bg);margin:calc(-1.1rem - .6rem - 1.5px) 0 .9rem .6rem;padding:0 .5rem;background:linear-gradient(var(--o),var(--o)) 0 50%/100% 4px no-repeat}`. The dlg class is added in the variant's plans.html and family.html.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r8-idea-2/form.png (New idea dialog); home.png (the ask box's double frame)
- Praise: System (8.2): 'dialogs are double-framed', and its fix asks to 'make the frame count carry meaning: one frame = window, two = dialog'. Style's fix: 'the real Turbo Vision convention to separate read from act'. Soul (8.2) regretted only that 'the typed-in field lost its halo'.
- Caveats: The frame's change of colour is subtle and invisible in stills. Keep the soul fix as well: the focused field gets a #6dff9c 1px ring plus a 12px bloom (`box-shadow:0 0 0 1px var(--lit),0 0 12px -3px color-mix(in srgb,var(--brand) 45%,transparent)`), so phosphor marks the focus and sage marks the form. Never put the double frame on read windows, or it reads as Bootstrap fieldsets. :has() needs a modern browser; without it the frame simply stays steel.
- Suits: All six. Flight Console: the form bay as an armed panel whose frame lights while a field is live. Videotex Switchboard: the keyboard ledge of the terminal lights while the family type. Night Timetable: the ask box, 'the stop you are at', lights while occupied. Pocket Timeline: the NOW box.

- What: Every small light on every page is one of four shapes with one meaning: a lamp (10px lit disc, white-hot core, two-layer halo) is on; a 7px solid steel dot (#93b4c8) is waiting; a 10px pewter ring (2px #8794a2) is off; a 12x5px amber bar needs a look, and turns red only once something has stopped. A one-line key ('● On · Waiting ○ Off ▬ Needs a look') sits where lamps mean something. Late on Home and To do is the amber bar before 'was due'.
- Files: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r13/variants/r13-mut-2/mapped.css section 2: .sig-lamp, .sig-dot, .sig-ring, .sig-bar, .sig-bar.red, where each stands, .sig-key (lines 16-68); forced-colours and more-contrast (139-149); templates/_ui.html macro signal(kind, tone) (183-185); templates/settings/messages.html.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/tour/gallery/r13-mut-2/status.png
- Praise: system notes: principle (1) 'worth writing into the standard'; system 8.1: 'Graft the four-signal rule and pewter off into the leader'; interaction 8.2: 'write them into the standard'; usability 8.2: 'late is never shown by colour alone'; lessons r13: 'Every lens wants this grafted'.
- Caveats: The pewter ring shares its shape with numbered band rings; told apart only by size and digit. A calm pewter ring can understate a real gap (no fallback model): keep a red ring for a gap that blocks answering. Latched-key LEDs as full lamps add bright points.
- Suits: All six. console: the annunciator's vocabulary. track: the lamp at each stop. switchboard: each cell's lamp. pocket: NOW, NEXT and LATER stops. timetable: stops on her line.

### L261 Linen ink ladder at 12:1 (typography)
From **r2-idea-4 Blue Hour**, round 2 (6.8, #3 of 12).
- What: The words are a warm linen instead of a cold mint-white, in one warm ladder: ink #d2ccbf (12.1:1 on bg), ink-2 #b3aea2, dim #96938a, faint #88857c. The calm comes from the text, while #6dff9c and the black stay exact, and the phosphor becomes the only green light because the words no longer lean green. Soft Ground (round 1, #e3dcd1 at 12.7:1) found the same thing first: warm ink makes the green stand apart from the text.
- Files: Palette: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r2/palettes/r2-idea-4.json (ink, ink-2, dim, faint); /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/round1/palettes/01-softground.json (ink #e3dcd1, ink-2 #beb9af). Sheet: the :root tokens of rounds/r2/out/r2-idea-4/style.css.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r2/out/r2-idea-4/shots/chat.png
- Praise: Feedback 7.6: "This answers 'too bright and contrasty' best of any palette that keeps the brand exact. Linen ink is at 12.1:1." Feedback notes: "the palettes that moved ink contrast into the 12s ... read noticeably calmer. Those that stayed at 13.1 to 13.6 read like today with a tint." Round 2 lessons: "Calm is measured in the ink ... Aim near 12:1 (ink #d2ccbf, ink-2 #b3aea2, dim #96938a)."
- Caveats: Its faint reached only 4.92:1, the lowest of the round; raise faint and dim a step so the smallest text clears 5:1. Pulling the ink down compresses the ladder (Soft Ground: 12.7 / 8.9 / 6.3), so the steps need size and weight to carry the hierarchy, not colour alone. Pushed warmer, linen yellows beside the lemon to-do colour.
- Suits: All six, as the text colour of the type system. Timetable: warm white type under enamel signs. Figure Ground: the manual's reading text against its blue drawing. Console: legends and readouts off the lit tubes. Track, Pocket and Switchboard: anywhere words sit beside lamps, so the lamps are the only green.

### L263 Legend set into the frame line (typography)
From **r5-idea-2 Front Panel**, round 5 (7.31, #2 of 12 in round 5 (6.67, #3 of 12 in round 6)).
- What: Each group is a 1px silk-screened outline, and its head is a condensed, tracked caps legend cut into the outline's top line, the way a fieldset legend or a bench instrument's panel print sits. The icon is tinted in the section's colour, and the 'All plans' or 'Earlier / Later' keys sit in the same line at the right. The legend row is 44px tall and pulled up by calc(-1.375rem - 0.5px) so the line runs through its middle; each child sits on a patch of ground colour that breaks the line. On Plans the month is one framed group with SEPTEMBER 2026 and its keys in the frame; on Status the three columns are framed groups with WHO ANSWERS, KEYS and CONNECTED TO as legends.

Markup in variants/r5-idea-2/templates/home.html, status.html, plans_month.html, tasks.html, settings.html, _settings.html, settings/general.html, idea_form.html, chat.html (class `group` and `legend`). Built sheet: rounds/r5/out/r5-idea-2/style.css.
- Shot: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-FamilyDB/62374e35-c7e3-5c25-a94d-0502bde8e9d6/scratchpad/harness/rounds/r5/out/r5-idea-2/shots/plans.png (month legend with Earlier/Later keys in the line); also shots/status.png
- Praise: Type lens, round 5, 7.1: 'The best new typographic device of the round: condensed, tracked caps legends set into the frame line, like fieldset legends silk-screened on a panel.' Type lens, round 6, 7.0: 'the most period-true typographic device here, and they hold on every page … Keep the legends exactly as they are; they are the trait to breed.' Style 7.8: 'Group names are printed into their frame lines like the silk-screened labels on a bench instrument.'
- Caveats: The patch is painted var(--bg), so it shows as a black blot on any slate or glass ground: use a `--legend-bg` token set on each container (system fix). The caps shouted over card titles: drop to about .9rem/600, and the round 10 rule says a legend never ranks below the rows it governs, and links in the line stay sentence case. The groups were empty outlines on bare black, which drained Status: put a slate fill inside the frame. The main line carried rail legends in rounds 8-9 (Reverse Video, Signal Legend), but today's leaders (Desk Terminal) do not.
- Suits: Flight Console: the console bays' engraved legends cut into each bay's frame, with the readouts' keys in the same line. Videotex Switchboard: each page cell's number and name set into its frame, like a patch-panel legend, with the count at the right of the line. Figure Ground: the plate number and title in the plate's frame above its figure. Night Timetable: each timetable's line sign cut into its frame.

### L264 Label-over-data rows (typography)

Type, 7.4: "label-over-data (the date above the title) with tabular facts on the right. It is disciplined and new." System's fix: keep "label-over-data (mono label line above a sans title)". lessons.md lists "label-over-data rows" among the type traits the panel rewarded.
